, see Lemma
5.8
. This proves (
5.36
) and the lower bound (
5.37
) is implied by the operator bounds in Lemma
5.8
.
∎

5.2.2
Step two – estimating the cross terms

In the second step of the proof of Proposition
5.7
we estimate the cross terms that we obtain when the decomposition in (
5.25
) with
\Psi
and
\xi_{0}
in (
5.29
) is inserted into the left side of (
5.24
).

Lemma 5.13
.

Given
D_{0},D_{1}\geqslant 0
, there is
B_{0}>0
with the following properties. If, for some
0<B\leqslant B_{0}
, the wave function
\alpha\in{L^{2}(Q_{B}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\langle\alpha,[U^{*}(1-P)U+U(1-P)U^{*}]\alpha\rangle\leqslant D_{0}B\,\|\alpha\|_{2}^{2}+D_{1}B^{2},

then
\Psi
and
\xi_{0}
in (
5.29
) satisfy the estimates

\displaystyle\|\alpha\|_{2}^{2}\leqslant C\bigl(\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+D_{1}B^{2}\bigr)

(5.69)

and

\displaystyle\langle\Psi,AA^{*}(1-AA^{*})\Psi\rangle+\|\xi_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leqslant C\bigl(B\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+D_{1}B^{2}\bigr).

(5.70)

Furthermore, for any
\eta>0
we have

\displaystyle|\langle\xi_{0},[U(1-P)(1+\pi_{r}^{2})(1-P)U^{*}+U^{*}(1-P)(1+\pi_{r}^{2})(1-P)U]A^{*}\Psi\rangle|

\displaystyle\hskip-180.0pt\leqslant\eta\,\|\Pi\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+C\left(1+\eta^{-1}\right)\,\bigl(B\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+D_{1}B^{2}\bigr).

(5.71)

Proof.

We start by noting that
A\xi_{0}=0
implies
\langle\xi_{0},A^{*}\Psi\rangle=0
, and hence

\displaystyle\|\alpha\|_{2}^{2}=\|A^{*}\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+\|\xi_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leqslant\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+\|\xi_{0}\|_{2}^{2}.

(5.72)

We use
\alpha\in{L^{2}(Q_{B}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}
and
A(1-A^{*}A)A^{*}=AA^{*}(1-AA^{*})
to see that

\displaystyle D_{0}B\|\alpha\|_{2}^{2}+D_{1}B^{2}

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\langle\alpha,[U^{*}(1-P)U+U(1-P)U^{*}]\alpha\rangle=\langle\alpha,(1-A^{*}A)\alpha\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle A^{*}\Psi,(1-A^{*}A)A^{*}\Psi\rangle+\langle\xi_{0},(1-A^{*}A)A^{*}\Psi\rangle+\langle A^{*}\Psi,(1-A^{*}A)\xi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 230.0pt+\langle\xi_{0},(1-A^{*}A)\xi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\Psi,AA^{*}(1-AA^{*})\Psi\rangle+\|\xi_{0}\|_{2}^{2}.

(5.73)

From Lemma
5.8
we know that the first term on the right side is nonnegative and hence

\|\xi_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leqslant D_{0}B\|\alpha\|_{2}^{2}+D_{1}B^{2}.

Together with (
5.72
), this also proves
(1-D_{0}B)\|\alpha\|_{2}^{2}\leqslant\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+D_{1}B^{2}
, that is, (
5.69
). Finally, (
5.69
) and (
5.73
) prove (
5.70
).

Next we prove (
5.71
). Let us define

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}:=U^{*}(1-P)(1+\pi_{r}^{2})(1-P)U+U(1-P)(1+\pi_{r}^{2})(1-P)U^{*}

(5.74)

and consider
\langle\xi_{0},\mathcal{T}A^{*}\Psi\rangle
. We note that
A\xi_{0}=0
implies
PU\xi_{0}=0=PU^{*}\xi_{0}
, where the projection
P
is understood to act on the relative coordinate. In combination with (
5.47
) this allows us to see that

\displaystyle\langle\xi_{0},\mathcal{T}A^{*}\Psi\rangle

\displaystyle=\Bigl\langle\xi_{0},\Bigl[2\widetilde{\pi}_{r}^{2}+\frac{\Pi_{X}^{2}}{2}\Bigr]A^{*}\Psi\Bigr\rangle-\langle\xi_{0},(U^{*}+U)\pi_{r}^{2}\,\alpha_{*}AA^{*}\Psi\rangle

(5.75)

holds. We use (
5.40
) and (
5.41
) to commute
\Pi_{X}^{2}
in the first term on the right side of (
5.75
) to the right and find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\langle\xi_{0},\Pi_{X}^{2}A^{*}\Psi\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\langle\xi_{0},A^{*}\Pi_{X}^{2}\Psi\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt-\mathrm{i}\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\;\overline{\xi_{0}(X,r)}\;\sin\bigl(\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}\bigr)\;\mathbf{B}\wedge r\;\alpha_{*}(r)\;\Pi_{X}\Psi(X)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+\frac{1}{2}\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\;\overline{\xi_{0}(X,r)}\;\cos\bigl(\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}\bigr)\;|\mathbf{B}\wedge r|^{2}\alpha_{*}(r)\;\Psi(X).

(5.76)

The first term on the right side vanishes because
A\xi_{0}=0
. Similarly, we apply (
5.42
) and (
5.44
) to commute
\widetilde{\pi}_{r}^{2}
in the first term in (
5.75
) to the right and find

\displaystyle 2\langle\xi_{0},\widetilde{\pi}_{r}^{2}A^{*}\Psi\rangle

\displaystyle=2\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\;\overline{\xi_{0}(X,r)}\;\cos\bigl(\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}\bigr)\;p^{2}\alpha_{*}(r)\;\Psi(X)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt+2\mathrm{i}\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\,\overline{\xi_{0}(X,r)}\;\sin\bigl(\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}\bigr)\;p\alpha_{*}(r)\;\Pi_{X}\Psi(X).

(5.77)

When we combine
\pi_{r}^{2}\alpha_{*}(r)=p_{r}^{2}\alpha_{*}(r)+\frac{1}{4}|\mathbf{B}\wedge r|^{2}\alpha_{*}(r)
, which holds because
\alpha_{*}
is radial, (
5.75
), (
5.76
) and (
5.77
), we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\xi_{0},\mathcal{T}A^{*}\Psi\rangle

\displaystyle=2\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\;\overline{\xi_{0}(X,r)}\;\cos\bigl(\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}\bigr)\;\pi_{r}^{2}\alpha_{*}(r)\;(1-AA^{*})\Psi(X)

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt+2\mathrm{i}\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\;\overline{\xi_{0}(X,r)}\;\sin\bigl(\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}\bigr)\;\bigl[p-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{B}\wedge r\bigr]\alpha_{*}(r)\;\Pi_{X}\Psi(X).

Using Cauchy-Schwarz, we bound the absolute value of this by

\displaystyle|\langle\xi_{0},\mathcal{T}A^{*}\Psi\rangle|\leqslant 2\|\xi_{0}\|_{2}\;\bigl[\|\pi_{r}^{2}\alpha_{*}\|_{2}\;\|(1-AA^{*})\Psi\|_{2}+\bigl(\|\nabla\alpha_{*}\|_{2}+B\|\,|\cdot|\alpha_{*}\|_{2}\bigr)\|\Pi\Psi\|_{2}\bigr],

(5.78)

and with the decay properties of
\alpha_{*}
in (
A.1
) we see that the norms of
\alpha_{*}
on the right side are bounded uniformly in
0\leqslant B\leqslant B_{0}
. Moreover, Lemma
5.8
and (
5.70
) imply that there is a constant
c>0
such that

\|(1-AA^{*})\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\leqslant\langle\Psi,(1-AA^{*})\Psi\rangle\leqslant\frac{1}{c}\langle\Psi,AA^{*}(1-AA^{*})\Psi\rangle\leqslant C\bigl(B\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+D_{1}B^{2}\bigr).

(5.79)

For
\eta>0
we thus obtain

\displaystyle|\langle\xi_{0},\mathcal{T}A^{*}\Psi\rangle|\leqslant C\bigl[\eta\,\|\Pi\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+\eta^{-1}\,\|\xi_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+\bigl(B\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+D_{1}B^{2}\bigr)\bigr]

(5.80)

and an application of (
5.70
) proves the claim.
∎

5.2.3
Proof of Proposition
5.7

We recall the decomposition
\alpha=A^{*}\Psi+\xi_{0}
with
\Psi
and
\xi_{0}
in (
5.29
) as well as
\mathcal{T}
in (
5.74
). From (
5.24
) and (
5.69
) we know that

\displaystyle C\bigl(B\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+D_{1}B^{2}\bigr)\geqslant\langle A^{*}\Psi,\mathcal{T}A^{*}\Psi\rangle+2\Re\langle\xi_{0},\mathcal{T}A^{*}\Psi\rangle+\langle\xi_{0},\mathcal{T}\xi_{0}\rangle.

(5.81)

With the help of Lemma
5.11
, the identities
PU\xi_{0}=0=PU^{*}\xi_{0}
imply

\displaystyle\langle\xi_{0},\mathcal{T}\xi_{0}\rangle=\Bigl\langle\xi_{0},\bigl(2+\frac{\Pi_{X}^{2}}{2}+2\widetilde{\pi}_{r}^{2}\bigr)\xi_{0}\Bigr\rangle\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\,\|\xi_{0}\|_{H^{1}(Q_{B}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}^{2}.

(5.82)

Lemma
5.8
guarantees the existence of a constant
\rho>0
such that

\displaystyle AA^{*}(1-AA^{*})(1+\Pi^{2})\geqslant\rho\;\Pi^{2}.

Therefore, (
5.37
) implies

\displaystyle\langle A^{*}\Psi,\mathcal{T}A^{*}\Psi\rangle\geqslant 2\,\langle\Psi,AA^{*}(1-AA^{*})(1+\Pi^{2})\Psi\rangle\geqslant 2\rho\,\langle\Psi,\Pi^{2}\Psi\rangle.

(5.83)

To estimate the second term on the right side of (
5.81
), we note that
\mathcal{T}
is bounded from below by
U(1-P)U^{*}+U^{*}(1-P)U
. Therefore, we may apply Lemma
5.13
with
\eta=\frac{\rho}{2}
and find

\displaystyle 2\Re\langle\xi_{0},\mathcal{T}A^{*}\Psi\rangle\geqslant-2\,|\langle\xi_{0},\mathcal{T}A^{*}\Psi\rangle|\geqslant-\rho\,\|\Pi\Psi\|_{2}^{2}-C\bigl(B\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+D_{1}B^{2}\bigr).

In combination with (
5.81
), (
5.82
) and (
5.83
), we thus obtain

\displaystyle C\bigl(B\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+D_{1}B^{2}\bigr)\geqslant\rho\,\|\Pi\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\,\|\xi_{0}\|_{H^{1}(Q_{B}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}^{2}.

This proves (
5.26
).

5.3
Uniform estimate on
\|\Psi\|_{2}

Up to now we neglected the nonlinear term on the left side of (
5.21
). This term provides the inequality

\displaystyle\Tr\bigl[(\alpha^{*}\alpha)^{2}\bigr]\leqslant C\left(B\|\alpha\|_{2}^{2}+B^{2}\right).

(5.84)

In this section we will take this term and (
5.84
) into account and show that it can be combined with Proposition
5.7
to obtain a bound for
\|\Psi\|_{2}
. This will afterwards allow us to prove Theorem
5.1
.

Lemma 5.14
.

Given
D_{0}\geqslant 0
, there is
B_{0}>0
such that for all
0<B\leqslant B_{0}
the following holds. If the wave function
\alpha\in{L^{2}(Q_{B}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}
obeys (
5.21
) then
\Psi
in (
5.29
) satisfies

\displaystyle\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant CB.

(5.85)

Proof.

We recall the decomposition
\alpha=A^{*}\Psi+\xi_{0}
with
\Psi
and
\xi_{0}
in (
5.29
). Eq. (
5.84
) and an application of the triangle inequality imply

\displaystyle C\bigl(B\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+B^{2}\bigr)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}\geqslant\|\alpha\|_{4}\geqslant\|A^{*}\Psi\|_{4}-\|\xi_{0}\|_{4}.

(5.86)

Thus, it suffices to prove an upper bound for
\|\xi_{0}\|_{4}
and a lower bound for
\|A^{*}\Psi\|_{4}
. Our proof follows closely the proof of
[
19
, Eq. (5.48)]
.

Step 1.
Let us start with the upper bound on
\|\xi_{0}\|_{4}
. We claim the estimate

\displaystyle\|\xi_{0}\|_{4}\leqslant C\bigl(B^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}\|\Psi\|_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+B^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{8}}}\|\Psi\|_{2}+B^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\bigr).

(5.87)

To see this, we first use Hölder’s inequality to estimate
\|\xi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}\leqslant\|\xi_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\;\|\xi_{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}
. From Proposition
5.7
we know that
\|\xi_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leqslant C(B\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+B^{2})
, and it thus remains to prove a bound for
\|\xi_{0}\|_{\infty}
. We claim that for any
\nu>3

\displaystyle\|\xi_{0}\|_{\infty}\leqslant 1+C_{\nu}\,B^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}\,\|(1+|\cdot|)^{\nu}\alpha_{*}\|_{\nicefrac{{6}}{{5}}}\;\|\Psi\|_{6},

(5.88)

where the right side is finite by the decay properties of
\alpha_{*}
in (
A.1
). To prove (
5.88
), we first note that (
1.6
) implies
\|\alpha\|_{\infty}\leqslant 1
, and hence
\|\xi_{0}\|_{\infty}\leqslant 1+\|A^{*}\Psi\|_{\infty}
. We apply Lemma
4.1
(b) to
A^{*}\Psi
and obtain (
5.88
). We also combine (
2.7
) with Proposition
5.7
and obtain
\|\Psi\|_{6}\leqslant C(\|\Psi\|_{2}+B^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}})
. In combination, these considerations imply (
5.87
).

Step 2.
We claim that

\displaystyle\|A^{*}\Psi\|_{4}^{4}\geqslant\frac{1}{16}\;\|\widehat{\alpha}_{*}\|_{4}^{4}\;\|\Psi\|_{4}^{4}-C\bigl(B^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{8}}}\|\Psi\|_{2}+B^{\nicefrac{{5}}{{8}}}\bigr)^{4}

(5.89)

holds. To prove (
5.89
), we write
\|A^{*}\Psi\|_{4}^{4}=\tr((A^{*}\Psi)^{*}A^{*}\Psi)^{2}
. The fact that
\alpha_{*}
is real-valued implies

\displaystyle\|A^{*}\Psi\|_{4}^{4}

\displaystyle=\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}x\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}y\;\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}z\;\alpha_{*}(x-z)\overline{\left[\cos\bigl(\frac{x-z}{2}\Pi_{\frac{x+z}{2}}\bigr)\Psi\bigl(\frac{x+z}{2}\bigr)\right]}

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt\times\alpha_{*}(z-y)\left[\cos\bigl(\frac{z-y}{2}\Pi_{\frac{z+y}{2}}\bigr)\Psi\bigl(\frac{z+y}{2}\bigr)\right]\Bigr|^{2}.

We use
\cos(x)=1-2\sin^{2}(\frac{x}{2})
twice and find

\displaystyle\|A^{*}\Psi\|_{4}^{4}

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{T}_{*}-C\,(\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}),

(5.90)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{*}

\displaystyle:=\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}x\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}y\;\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}z\;\alpha_{*}(x-z)\overline{\Psi\bigl(\frac{x+z}{2}\bigr)}\alpha_{*}(z-y)\Psi\bigl(\frac{z+y}{2}\bigr)\Bigr|^{2}

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}x\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}y\;\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}z\;\alpha_{*}(x-z)\overline{\Psi\bigl(\frac{x+z}{2}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\hskip 140.0pt\times\alpha_{*}(z-y)\left[\sin^{2}\bigl(\frac{z-y}{4}\Pi_{\frac{z+y}{2}}\bigr)\Psi\bigl(\frac{z+y}{2}\bigr)\right]\Bigr|^{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}x\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}y\;\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}z\;\alpha_{*}(x-z)\overline{\left[\sin^{2}\bigl(\frac{x-z}{4}\Pi_{\frac{x+z}{2}}\bigr)\Psi\bigl(\frac{x+z}{2}\bigr)\right]}

\displaystyle\hskip 140.0pt\times\alpha_{*}(z-y)\left[\cos\bigl(\frac{z-y}{2}\Pi_{\frac{z+y}{2}}\bigr)\Psi\bigl(\frac{z+y}{2}\bigr)\right]\Bigr|^{2}.

(5.91)

In the following we derive a lower bound on
\mathcal{T}_{*}
and an upper bound on
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}
.

Lower bound on
\mathcal{T}_{*}
.
We change variables
z\mapsto z+x
and
y\mapsto y+x
and afterwards replace
x
by
X
, which allows us to write

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{*}=\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}y\;\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}z\;\alpha_{*}(z)\overline{\Psi\bigl(X+\frac{z}{2}\bigr)}\alpha_{*}(z-y)\Psi\bigl(X+\frac{z+y}{2}\bigr)\Bigr|^{2}.

(5.92)

Next, we combine
\Psi(X+\frac{z}{2})=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z}{2}P_{X}}\Psi(X)
and the identity
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{r}{2}P_{X}}=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathbf{B}}{2}\cdot(r\wedge X)}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}}
in (
5.39
) to write
\Psi(X+\frac{z}{2})=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathbf{B}}{2}\cdot(z\wedge X)}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z}{2}\Pi_{X}}\Psi(X)
.
We conclude that

\displaystyle\overline{\Psi\bigl(X+\frac{z}{2}\bigr)}\Psi\bigl(X+\frac{z+y}{2}\bigr)=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathbf{B}}{2}\cdot(y\wedge X)}\;\overline{\left[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z}{2}\Pi_{X}}\Psi(X)\right]}\;\left[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z+y}{2}\Pi_{X}}\Psi(X)\right],

as well as

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{*}=\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}y\;\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}z\;\alpha_{*}(-z)\overline{\left[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z}{2}\Pi_{X}}\Psi(X)\right]}\alpha_{*}(z-y)\left[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z+y}{2}\Pi_{X}}\Psi(X)\right]\Bigr|^{2}.

This also implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{*}\geqslant\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{T}_{*}^{*}-C(\mathcal{T}_{*}^{(1)}+\mathcal{T}_{*}^{(2)})

(5.93)

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{*}^{*}:=\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}y\;\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}z\;\alpha_{*}(z)\overline{\Psi(X)}\alpha_{*}(z-y)\Psi(X)\Bigr|^{2}=\|\Psi\|_{4}^{4}\;\|\alpha_{*}*\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{*}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}y\;\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}z\;\alpha_{*}(z)\overline{\bigl[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z}{2}\Pi_{X}}\Psi(X)\bigr]}\alpha_{*}(z-y)\bigl[\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z+y}{2}\Pi_{X}}-1\bigr)\Psi(X)\bigr]\Bigr|^{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{*}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}y\;\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}z\;\alpha_{*}(z)\overline{\bigl[\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z}{2}\Pi_{X}}-1\bigr)\Psi(X)\bigr]}\alpha_{*}(z-y)\Psi(X)\Bigr|^{2}.

(5.94)

Upper bound on
\mathcal{T}_{*}^{(1)}
and
\mathcal{T}_{*}^{(2)}
.
We start with
\mathcal{T}_{*}^{(1)}
, expand the square and estimate

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{*}^{(1)}\leqslant\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}y\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}z\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\;|\alpha_{*}(z)\,\alpha_{*}(z^{\prime})\,\alpha_{*}(z-y)\,\alpha_{*}(z^{\prime}-y)|

(5.95)

\displaystyle\times\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\;\Bigl|\bigl[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z}{2}\Pi_{X}}\Psi(X)\bigr]\bigl[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z^{\prime}}{2}\Pi_{X}}\Psi(X)\bigr]\bigl[\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z+y}{2}\Pi_{X}}-1\bigr)\Psi(X)\bigr]\bigl[\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z^{\prime}+y}{2}\Pi_{X}}-1\bigr)\Psi(X)\bigr]\Bigr|.

When we use Hölder’s inequality, (
4.124
), (
4.99
), and (
2.7
), we see that the integral in the second line can be bounded by

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z}{2}\Pi}\Psi\|_{6}^{2}\;\|(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z+y}{2}\Pi}-1)\Psi\|_{6}\;\|(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z+y}{2}\Pi}-1)\Psi\|_{2}\leqslant C\;\bigl|\frac{z+y}{2}\bigr|\;B^{-\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}\;\|\Pi\Psi\|_{2}^{4}.

(5.96)

Proposition
5.7
provides us with a bound for
\|\Pi\Psi\|_{2}
. In combination with (
5.95
), (
5.96
), Young’s inequality, and the bound
|z+y|\leqslant 2|z|+|z-y|
, this implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{*}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,B^{-\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}\,\left(B^{2}\|\Psi\|_{2}^{4}+D_{1}B^{4}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}y\;\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}z\;|z+y|\;|\alpha_{*}(z)\alpha_{*}(y-z)|\Bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(B^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\|\Psi\|_{2}^{4}+D_{1}B^{\nicefrac{{5}}{{2}}}\right)\,\|\alpha_{*}\|_{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}}\,\|\,|\cdot|\alpha_{*}\|_{\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}},

(5.97)

where the right side is finite by (
A.1
). Similarly, we see that
\mathcal{T}_{*}^{(2)}
is bounded by the right side of (
5.97
).

Upper bound on
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}
in (
5.91
).
Bounds for
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}
can be obtain along the same lines as the bound for
\mathcal{T}_{*}^{(1)}
. We apply the same change of variables as above and use estimates similar to the ones in (
5.95
). In case of
\mathcal{T}_{1}
, the bound in (
5.96
) needs to be replaced by

\displaystyle\|\Psi\|_{6}^{2}\,\bigl\|\sin^{2}\bigl(\frac{z-y}{2}\Pi\bigr)\Psi\bigr\|_{6}\,\bigl\|\sin^{2}\bigl(\frac{z-y}{2}\Pi\bigr)\Psi\bigr\|_{2}\leqslant C\;\frac{|z-y|}{2}\;B^{-\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}\,\|\Pi\Psi\|_{2}^{4}.

(5.98)

Here, we used
\sin^{2}(x)\leqslant|x|
and the operator inequality in (
4.99
) to estimate the third factor. For the first and the second factor, we used

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\bigl(\frac{z-y}{4}\Pi\bigr)=-\frac{1}{4}\bigl(2+\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{z-y}{2}\Pi}+\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{z-y}{2}\Pi}\bigr)

and (
2.7
) or (
4.124
), respectively. A bound for
\mathcal{T}_{2}
can be proved analogously. The final estimate we obtain in this way reads

\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}\leqslant C\left(B^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\|\Psi\|_{2}^{4}+D_{1}B^{\nicefrac{{5}}{{2}}}\right).

(5.99)

In combination with (
5.90
), (
5.93
), and (
5.97
), this proves (
5.89
).

Step 3.
We denote
c:=\frac{1}{2}\|\widehat{\alpha}_{*}\|_{4}
, insert (
5.87
) and (
5.89
) into (
5.86
) and obtain

\displaystyle CB^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\Psi\|_{2}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\geqslant c\|\Psi\|_{4}-CB^{\frac{1}{8}}\|\Psi\|_{2}-CB^{\frac{1}{2}},

(5.100)

which holds for
B
small enough. For
\eta>0
the left side is bounded from above by a constant times
\eta\|\Psi\|_{2}+\eta^{-1}B^{\frac{1}{2}}
and Hölder’s inequality implies
\|\Psi\|_{4}\geqslant\|\Psi\|_{2}
.
Accordingly,

C\left(\eta\|\Psi\|_{2}+\eta^{-1}B^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\geqslant(c-CB^{\frac{1}{8}})\|\Psi\|_{2}-CB^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.101)

When we choose
\eta
and
B
in (
5.101
) small enough, this proves the claim.
∎

5.4
Proof of Theorem
5.1

The assumption (
5.1
) in Theorem
5.1
, Corollary
5.6
, Proposition
5.7
, and Lemma
5.14
imply the decomposition
\alpha=A^{*}\Psi+\xi_{0}
, where
\Psi
and
\xi_{0}
in (
5.29
) obey

\displaystyle\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}=B^{-1}\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+B^{-2}\|\Pi\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\leqslant C

(5.102)

and

\displaystyle\|\xi_{0}\|_{{H^{1}(Q_{B}\times\mathbb{R}_{\mathrm{s}}^{3})}}^{2}\leqslant CB^{2}\bigl(\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}+D_{1}\bigr).

(5.103)

Define

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle:=\xi_{0}+\bigl(\cos\bigl(\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}\bigr)-1\bigr)\alpha_{*}(r)\Psi(X).

(5.104)

Then, (
5.2
) holds and we claim that
\xi
satisfies (
5.3
). To prove this, we estimate the second term in (
5.104
). We use
1-\cos(x)\leqslant|x|
and (
4.99
) to bound

\displaystyle\bigl\|\bigl(\cos\bigl(\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}\bigr)-1\bigr)\Psi\alpha_{*}\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\;\||\cdot|\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\;\|\Pi\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\leqslant CB^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2},

where the right side is finite by the decay properties of
\alpha_{*}
in (
A.1
). Using additionally (
5.40
), we also see that

\displaystyle\bigl\|\Pi_{X}\bigl(\cos\bigl(\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}\bigr)-1\bigr)\alpha_{*}\Psi\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\bigl\|\bigl[\cos\bigl(\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}\bigr)-1\bigr]\Pi_{X}\alpha_{*}\Psi\bigr\|_{2}^{2}+\bigl\|\sin\bigl(\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}\bigr)\mathbf{B}\wedge r\alpha_{*}\Psi\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\bigl(\|\Pi\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+B^{2}\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)\leqslant CB^{3}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}

holds. Finally, an application of (
5.43
) and (
4.99
) allows us to estimate

\displaystyle\bigl\|\pi_{r}\bigl(\cos\bigl(\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}\bigr)-1\bigr)\Psi\alpha_{*}\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|\bigl[\bigl(\cos\bigl(\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}\bigr)-1\bigr)\widetilde{\pi}_{r}+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\sin\bigl(\frac{r}{2}\Pi_{X}\bigr)\Pi_{X}\bigr]\Psi\alpha_{*}\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\bigl(\|\Pi\Psi\|_{2}^{2}+B^{2}\|\Psi\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)\leqslant CB^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}.

This proves that
\xi
obeys (
5.3
) and ends the proof of Theorem
5.1
.

6
The Lower Bound on (
1.21
) and Proof of Theorem
2
(b)

6.1
The BCS energy of low-energy states

In this section, we provide the lower bound on (
1.21
) and the proof of Theorem
2
(b), and thereby complete the proof of Theorems
1
and
2
. Let
D_{1}\geqslant 0
and
D\in\mathbb{R}
be given and assume that
\Gamma
is a gauge-periodic state at temperature
T=T_{\mathrm{c}}(1-DB)
that satisfies (
5.1
).
Corollary
5.2
provides us with a decomposition of the Cooper pair wave function
\alpha=[\Gamma]_{12}
in terms of
\Psi_{\leqslant}
in (
5.4
) and
\sigma
in (
5.9
), where
\|\Psi_{\leqslant}\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}\leqslant C
and where the bound

\displaystyle\|\Psi_{\leqslant}\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{2}(Q_{B})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\varepsilon B^{-1}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}

(6.1)

holds in terms of the function
\Psi
in Theorem
5.1
. With the function
\Psi_{\leqslant}
we construct a Gibbs state
\Gamma_{\Delta}
with the gap function
\Delta\equiv\Delta_{\Psi_{\leqslant}}
as in (
3.1
). Using Proposition
3.4
, we write the BCS free energy of
\Gamma
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{\mathbf{B},T}(\Gamma)-\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{\mathbf{B},T}(\Gamma_{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}\langle\Delta,L_{T,B}\Delta\rangle+\frac{1}{8}\langle\Delta,N_{T,B}(\Delta)\rangle+\|\Psi_{\leqslant}\|_{2}^{2}\;\langle\alpha_{*},V\alpha_{*}\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+\Tr\bigl[\mathcal{R}_{T,B}(\Delta)\bigr]+\frac{T}{2}\mathcal{H}_{0}(\Gamma,\Gamma_{\Delta})-\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\;V(r)\,|\sigma(X,r)|^{2},

where

\|\mathcal{R}_{T,B}(\Delta)\|_{1}\leqslant C\;B^{3}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{6}.

We also apply Theorem
3.5
to compute the terms in the first line on the right side, and find the lower bound

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{\mathbf{B},T}(\Gamma)-\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{\mathbf{B},T}(\Gamma_{0})

\displaystyle\geqslant B^{2}\;\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{GL}}_{D,B}(\Psi_{\leqslant})-C\bigl(B^{3}+\varepsilon B^{2}\bigr)\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt+\frac{T}{2}\mathcal{H}_{0}(\Gamma,\Gamma_{\Delta})-\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\;V(r)\,|\sigma(X,r)|^{2}.

(6.2)

The relative entropy is nonnegative and the last term on the right side is nonpositive. In the next section we show that their sum is negligible.

6.2
Estimate on the relative entropy

In this section we prove a lower bound for the second line in (
6.2
), showing that it is negligible. We start with the following lower bound for the relative entropy.

Lemma 6.1
.

For all admissible BCS states
\Gamma
, we have

\displaystyle T\mathcal{H}_{0}(\Gamma,\Gamma_{\Delta})

\displaystyle\geqslant\Tr\Bigl[(\Gamma-\Gamma_{\Delta})\frac{H_{\Delta}}{\tanh(\frac{\beta}{2}H_{\Delta})}(\Gamma-\Gamma_{\Delta})\Bigr].

(6.3)

Proof.

The proof is given in
[
19
, Lemma 5]
and uses the fact that
\Gamma_{\Delta}
is admissible, which follows from Lemma
3.1
.
∎

To be able to combine the term on the right side of (
6.3
) and the last term on the right side of (
6.2
), we first need to replace the operator
H_{\Delta}
in the second factor on the right side of (
6.3
) by
H_{0}
. To that end, we note that the estimate
H_{\Delta}^{2}\geqslant(1-\delta)H_{0}^{2}-\delta^{-1}\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{2}
holds for
0<\delta<1
and we rewrite it as

\displaystyle H_{0}\leqslant(1-\delta)^{-1}\bigl(H_{\Delta}^{2}+\delta^{-1}\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{2}\bigr).

(6.4)

Furthermore, we note that the series expansion

\displaystyle\frac{x}{\tanh(\frac{x}{2})}=2+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\bigl(2-\frac{8k^{2}\pi^{2}}{x^{2}+4k^{2}\pi^{2}}\bigr),

see
[
19
, Eq. (5.14)]
, shows that the function
x\mapsto\frac{\sqrt{x}}{\tanh(\frac{\beta}{2}\sqrt{x})}
is operator monotone. We use this together with (
6.4
) and the monotonicity of the map
x\mapsto\tanh(x)
, which yields

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}K_{T,B}&0\\
0&\overline{K_{T,B}}\end{pmatrix}=\frac{H_{0}}{\tanh(\frac{\beta}{2}H_{0})}

\displaystyle\leqslant(1-\delta)^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{\sqrt{H_{\Delta}^{2}+\delta^{-1}\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{2}}}{\tanh(\frac{\beta}{2}\sqrt{H_{\Delta}^{2}+\delta^{-1}\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{2}})}.

When we apply a first order Taylor expansion on the right side, the above inequality can be written as

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}K_{T,B}&0\\
0&\overline{K_{T,B}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\leqslant(1-\delta)^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Bigl[\frac{H_{\Delta}}{\tanh(\frac{\beta}{2}H_{\Delta})}+\frac{\beta}{4}\int_{0}^{\delta^{-1}\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{2}}\mathrm{d}t\;g\bigl(\frac{\beta}{2}\sqrt{H_{\Delta}^{2}+t}\bigr)\Bigr]

with the nonnegative function

\displaystyle g(x)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{x}\frac{1}{\tanh(x)}-\frac{1}{\tanh^{2}(x)}\frac{1}{\cosh^{2}(x)}.

Using
\sup_{x\geqslant 0}g(x)\leqslant 1
and
1\leqslant\frac{x}{\tanh(x)}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}K_{T,B}&0\\
0&\overline{K_{T,B}}\end{pmatrix}\leqslant(1-\delta)^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\bigl(1+\frac{\delta^{-1}\beta^{2}}{8}\;\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{2}\bigr)\frac{H_{\Delta}}{\tanh(\frac{\beta}{2}H_{\Delta})}

holds. We choose
\delta:=\|\Delta\|_{\infty}
and note that Lemma
4.1
and (
2.7
) imply

\|\Delta\|_{\infty}\leqslant C\;B^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}.

(6.5)

In particular,
\delta<1
as long as
B>0
is sufficiently small, and we have

\displaystyle\frac{H_{\Delta}}{\tanh(\frac{\beta}{2}H_{\Delta})}\geqslant\frac{\sqrt{1-\|\Delta\|_{\infty}}}{1+\frac{\beta^{2}}{8}\|\Delta\|_{\infty}}\;\begin{pmatrix}K_{T,B}&0\\
0&\overline{K_{T,B}}\end{pmatrix}\geqslant(1-C\|\Delta\|_{\infty})\;\begin{pmatrix}K_{T,B}&0\\
0&\overline{K_{T,B}}\end{pmatrix}.

(6.6)

In combination, (
6.3
) and (
6.6
) prove

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Tr\Bigl[(\Gamma-\Gamma_{\Delta})\frac{H_{\Delta}}{\tanh(\frac{\beta}{2}H_{\Delta})}(\Gamma-\Gamma_{\Delta})\Bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip-160.0pt\geqslant(1-C\|\Delta\|_{\infty})\langle\alpha-\alpha_{\Delta},K_{T,B}(\alpha-\alpha_{\Delta})\rangle+(1-C\|\Delta\|_{\infty})\Tr[(\gamma-\gamma_{\Delta})K_{T,B}(\gamma-\gamma_{\Delta})],

where we can drop the last term for a lower bound because it is nonnegative if
B
is sufficiently small. This is the lower bound for the relative entropy of
\Gamma
with respect to
\Gamma_{\Delta}
we were looking for.

It remains to combine the first term on the right side and the interaction term on the right side of (
6.2
).
Let us define the function
\eta:=\alpha_{*}\Psi_{\leqslant}-\alpha_{\Delta}
. By Corollary
5.2
we have
\alpha-\alpha_{\Delta}=\sigma+\eta
as well as

\displaystyle\frac{T}{2}\mathcal{H}_{0}(\Gamma,\Gamma_{\Delta})-\fint_{Q_{B}}\mathrm{d}X\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\;V(r)|\sigma(X,r)|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt\geqslant(1-C\|\Delta\|_{\infty})\langle\sigma+\eta,K_{T,B}(\sigma+\eta)\rangle-\langle\sigma,V\sigma\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 20.0pt\geqslant(1-C\|\Delta\|_{\infty})\langle\sigma,(K_{T,B}-V)\sigma\rangle-C\|\Delta\|_{\infty}\|V\|_{\infty}\|\sigma\|_{2}^{2}-2\,|\langle\eta,K_{T,B}\sigma\rangle|.

(6.7)

From (
5.14
) we know that the lowest eigenvalue of
K_{T,B}-V
is bounded from below by
-CB
. In combination with (
5.10
) and (
6.5
), this implies that the first two terms on the right side of (
6.7
) are bounded from below by

\displaystyle-C\varepsilon^{-1}B^{\nicefrac{{9}}{{4}}}\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}\;\bigl(\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}+D_{1}\bigr)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.

(6.8)

To estimate the last term on the right side of (
6.7
), we use (
5.15
) to replace
K_{T,B}
by
K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}
, which yields the estimate

\displaystyle|\langle\eta,(K_{T,B}-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B})\sigma\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2D_{0}B\,\|\sigma\|_{2}\,\|\eta\|_{2}\leqslant C\,B^{3}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}\;\bigl(\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}+D_{1}\bigr)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.

To obtain this result we also used (
5.10
),
Proposition
3.2
and (
6.1
). Next, we decompose
\eta=\eta_{0}+\eta_{\perp}
with
\eta_{0}(\Delta)
and
\eta_{\perp}(\Delta)
as in Proposition
3.2
and write

\displaystyle\langle\eta,K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}\sigma\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\eta_{0},K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}\sigma\rangle+\langle\eta_{\perp},K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}(\sigma-\sigma_{0})\rangle+\langle\eta_{\perp},K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}\sigma_{0}\rangle.

(6.9)

Using (
3.7
) and (
5.10
), we see that the first term on the right side of (
6.9
) is bounded by

\displaystyle|\langle\eta_{0},K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}\sigma\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant\bigl\|\sqrt{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}}\,\eta_{0}\bigr\|_{2}\bigl\|\sqrt{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}}\,\sigma\bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}B^{\nicefrac{{5}}{{2}}}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}\;\bigl(\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}+D_{1}\bigr)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.

(6.10)

We note that
\sigma-\sigma_{0}=\xi
and use (
3.8
), (
5.3
), and (
6.1
) to estimate

\displaystyle|\langle\eta_{\perp},K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}\xi\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant\bigl\|\sqrt{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}}\,\eta_{\perp}\bigr\|_{2}\bigl\|\sqrt{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}}\,\xi\bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}B^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}\;\bigl(\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}+D_{1}\bigr)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.

(6.11)

It remains to estimate the last term on the right side of (
6.9
), which we write as

\displaystyle\langle\eta_{\perp},K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}\sigma_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\eta_{\perp},K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{r}\sigma_{0}\rangle+\langle\eta_{\perp},[K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{r}]\sigma_{0}\rangle+\langle\eta_{\perp},(U-1)K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}\sigma_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 190.0pt+\langle\eta_{\perp},UK_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}(U^{*}-1)\sigma_{0}\rangle.

(6.12)

with the unitary operator
U
in (
5.20
). We recall that the operators
K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}
and
K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{r}
act on the relative coordinate
r=x-y
.

Since
\Delta(X,r)=-2V(r)\alpha_{*}(r)\Psi_{\leqslant}(X)
and
\sigma_{0}(X,r)=\alpha_{*}(r)\Psi_{>}(X)
we know from Proposition
3.2
(c) that the first term on the right side of (
6.12
) vanishes. A bound for the remaining terms is provided by the following lemma. Its proof will be given in Section
6.4
below.

Lemma 6.2
.

We have the following estimates on the remainder terms of (
6.12
):

(a)

|\langle\eta_{\perp},[K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{r}]\sigma_{0}\rangle|\leqslant CB^{3}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}
,

(b)

|\langle\eta_{\perp},(U-1)K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}\sigma_{0}\rangle|\leqslant C\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}B^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}
,

(c)

|\langle\eta_{\perp},UK_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}(U^{*}-1)\sigma_{0}\rangle|\leqslant C\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}B^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}
.

Accordingly, we have

\displaystyle|\langle\sigma,K_{T,B}\eta\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\bigl(\varepsilon^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}B^{\nicefrac{{5}}{{2}}}+\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}B^{2}\bigr)\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}\;\bigl(\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}+D_{1}\bigr)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.

We combine this with (
6.2
), (
6.7
), and (
6.8
) to see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{\mathbf{B},T}(\Gamma)-\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{\mathbf{B},T}(\Gamma_{0})

\displaystyle\geqslant B^{2}\;\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{GL}}_{D,B}(\Psi_{\leqslant})

\displaystyle\hskip-70.0pt-C\bigl(\varepsilon^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}B^{\nicefrac{{5}}{{2}}}+\varepsilon^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}B^{2}+\varepsilon^{-1}B^{\nicefrac{{9}}{{4}}}\bigr)\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}\bigl(\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}+D_{1}\bigr)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.

(6.13)

The optimal choice
\varepsilon=B^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{6}}}
in (
6.13
) yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{\mathbf{B},T}(\Gamma)-\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{\mathbf{B},T}(\Gamma_{0})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\geqslant B^{2}\,\bigl(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{GL}}_{D,B}(\Psi_{\leqslant})-C\,B^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{12}}}\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}\;\bigl(\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}+D_{1}\bigr)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\bigr).

(6.14)

This proves the lower bound for the BCS free energy in Theorem
1
.

6.3
Conclusion

Using (
6.14
), we now finish the proofs of Theorem
1
and Theorem
2
, and we start with the former. Let
\Gamma
be an approximate minimizer of the BCS functional, i.e., let (
5.1
) hold with

\displaystyle D_{1}:=E^{\mathrm{GL}}(D)+\rho

(6.15)

and
\rho\geqslant 0
. Since
\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}\leqslant C
by (
5.3
), (
6.14
) implies

\displaystyle B^{2}\bigl(E^{\mathrm{GL}}(D)+\rho\bigr)\geqslant\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{\mathbf{B},T}(\Gamma)-\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{\mathbf{B},T}(\Gamma_{0})\geqslant B^{2}\bigl(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{GL}}_{D,B}(\Psi_{\leqslant})-C\,B^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{12}}}\bigr).

This proves the claimed bound for the Cooper pair wave function of an approximate minimizer of the BCS functional in Theorem
1
.

We turn to the proof of Theorem
2
. Let the temperature
T
obey

\displaystyle T_{\mathrm{c}}(1-B(D_{\mathrm{c}}-D_{0}B^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{12}}}))<T\leqslant T_{\mathrm{c}}(1+CB)

(6.16)

with
D_{\mathrm{c}}
in (
1.20
) and
D_{0}>0
. We claim that the normal state
\Gamma_{0}
minimizes the BCS functional for such temperatures
T
if
D_{0}
is chosen sufficiently large. Since Corollary
5.5
takes care of the remaining temperature range, this implies part (b) of Theorem
2
and completes its proof.

To see that the above claim is true, we start with the lower bound in (
6.14
) and assume that (
5.1
) holds with
D_{1}=0
. We drop the nonnegative quartic term in the Ginzburg–Landau functional for a lower bound and obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{GL}}_{D,B}(\Psi_{\leqslant})

\displaystyle\geqslant B^{-2}\langle\Psi_{\leqslant},(\Lambda_{0}\,\Pi^{2}-DB\Lambda_{2})\Psi_{\leqslant}\rangle\geqslant\Lambda_{2}\,(D_{\mathrm{c}}-D)\;B^{-1}\|\Psi_{\leqslant}\|_{2}^{2},

with
\Lambda_{0}
in (
3.22
),
\Lambda_{2}
in (
3.23
), and with
D\in\mathbb{R}
defined by
T=T_{\mathrm{c}}(1-DB)
. We combine (
5.6
) and (
5.26
) and estimate

\displaystyle\|\Psi_{\leqslant}\|_{2}\geqslant\|\Psi\|_{2}-\|\Psi_{>}\|_{2}\geqslant c\;B^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}(1-C\,B^{\nicefrac{{5}}{{12}}}).

When we insert our findings in the lower bound for the BCS energy in (
6.14
), this gives

0\geqslant\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{\mathbf{B},T}(\Gamma)-\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{\mathbf{B},T}(\Gamma_{0})\geqslant c\;B^{2}\;\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}\bigl((D_{\mathrm{c}}-D)-CB^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{12}}}\bigr),

(6.17)

We note that the lower bound in (
6.16
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{c}}-D>D_{0}B^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{12}}}.

(6.18)

When we choose
D_{0}>C
with
C>0
in (
6.17
) and use (
6.18
) to obtain a lower bound for the right side of (
6.17
), we conclude that
\Psi=0
. By (
5.2
) and (
5.3
), this implies that
\alpha=0
whence
\Gamma
is a diagonal state. Therefore,
\Gamma_{0}
is the unique minimizer of
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{BCS}}_{\mathbf{B},T}
if
T
satisfies (
6.16
) with our choice of
D_{0}
. As explained below (
6.16
), this proves Theorem
2
.

6.4
Proof of Lemma
6.2

In this section we prove Lemma
6.2
.
Our proof of part (a) uses a Cauchy integral representation for the operator
K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-(\pi^{2}-\mu)
, which is provided in Lemma
6.4
below. Let us start by defining the contour for the Cauchy integral.

Definition 6.3

(Speaker path)
.

Let
R>0
, assume that
\mu\geqslant-1
and define the following complex paths

\displaystyle\begin{split}u_{1}(t)&:=\frac{\pi\mathrm{i}}{2\beta_{\mathrm{c}}}+(1+\mathrm{i})t\\
u_{2}(t)&:=\frac{\pi\mathrm{i}}{2\beta_{\mathrm{c}}}-(\mu+1)t\\
u_{3}(t)&:=-\frac{\pi\mathrm{i}}{2\beta_{\mathrm{c}}}t-(\mu+1)\\
u_{4}(t)&:=-\frac{\pi\mathrm{i}}{2\beta_{\mathrm{c}}}-(\mu+1)(1-t)\\
u_{5}(t)&:=-\frac{\pi\mathrm{i}}{2\beta_{\mathrm{c}}}+(1-\mathrm{i})t\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phantom{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\beta_{\mathrm{c}}}}t&\in[0,R],\\
\phantom{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\beta_{\mathrm{c}}}}t&\in[0,1],\\
\phantom{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\beta_{\mathrm{c}}}}t&\in[-1,1],\\
\phantom{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\beta_{\mathrm{c}}}}t&\in[0,1],\\
\phantom{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\beta_{\mathrm{c}}}}t&\in[0,R].\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{\includegraphics[width]{Speaker_path.pdf}}\end{split}

The speaker path is defined as the union of paths
u_{i}
,
i=1,\ldots,5
, with
u_{1}
taken in reverse direction, i.e.,

\displaystyle{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}_{R}:=\mathop{\dot{-}}u_{1}\mathop{\dot{+}}u_{2}\mathop{\dot{+}}u_{3}\mathop{\dot{+}}u_{4}\mathop{\dot{+}}u_{5}.

If
\mu<-1
we choose the same path as in the case
\mu=-1
.

This path has the property that certain norms of the resolvent kernel of
\pi^{2}
are uniformly bounded for
z\in{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}_{R}
and
R>0
. More precisely, Lemma
4.5
implies

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leqslant B\leqslant B_{0}}\sup_{R>0}\sup_{w\in{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}_{R}}\bigl[\left\|\,|\cdot|^{a}g_{B}^{w}\right\|_{1}+\left\|\,|\cdot|^{a}\nabla g_{B}^{w}\right\|_{1}\bigr]<\infty.

(6.19)

We could also choose a path parallel to the real axis in Lemma
6.4
below. In this case the above norms would depend on
R
. Although our analysis also works in this case, we decided to use the path
{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}_{R}
because of the more elegant bound in (
6.19
).
With the above definition at hand, we are prepared to state the following lemma.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
H\colon\mathcal{D}(H)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
be a self-adjoint operator on a separable Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
with
H\geqslant-\mu
and let
\beta>0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\frac{H}{\tanh(\frac{\beta}{2}H)}=H+\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}_{R}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\Bigl(\frac{z}{\tanh(\frac{\beta}{2}z)}-z\Bigr)\frac{1}{z-H},

(6.20)

with the speaker path
{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}_{R}
in Definition
6.3
. The above integral including the limit is understood as an improper Riemann integral with respect to the uniform operator topology.

Proof.

We have that the function
f(z)=\frac{z}{\tanh(\frac{\beta}{2}z)}-z=\frac{2z}{\mathrm{e}^{\beta z}-1}
is analytic in the open domain
\mathbb{C}\setminus 2\pi T\mathrm{i}\mathbb{Z}_{\neq 0}
. The construction of the Riemann integral over the path
{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}_{R}
with respect to the uniform operator topology is standard. The fact that the limit
R\to\infty
exists in the same topology follows from the exponential decay of the function
f(z)
along the speaker path. To check the equality in (
6.20
), we evaluate both sides in the inner product with two vectors in
\ran\mathbbs 1_{(-\infty,K]}(H)
for
K>0
, use the functional calculus, the Cauchy integral formula, and the fact that
\bigcup_{K>0}\ran\mathbbs 1_{(-\infty,K]}(H)
is a dense subset of
\mathcal{H}
. This proves the claim.
∎

Henceforth, we use the symbol
\int_{{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}}
to denote the integral on the right side of (
6.20
) including the limit and we denote
{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}=\bigcup_{R>0}{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}_{R}
.

Proof of Lemma
6.2
.

We apply Cauchy-Schwarz to estimate

\displaystyle|\langle\eta_{\perp},(K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{r})\sigma_{0}\rangle|\leqslant\|\eta_{\perp}\|_{2}\,\|(K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{r})\sigma_{0}\|_{2}

(6.21)

and claim that

\displaystyle\|[K_{{T_{\mathrm{c}}},B}^{r}-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{r}]\sigma_{0}\|_{2}\leqslant C\varepsilon^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}B^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}

(6.22)

holds. To see this, we apply Lemma
6.4
and write

\displaystyle K_{{T_{\mathrm{c}}},B}^{r}-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{r}=\pi_{r}^{2}-p_{r}^{2}+\int_{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}\frac{\mathrm{d}w}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\;f(w)\;\frac{1}{w+\mu-\pi_{r}^{2}}[\pi_{r}^{2}-p_{r}^{2}]\frac{1}{w+\mu-p_{r}^{2}},

(6.23)

where
\pi_{r}^{2}-p_{r}^{2}=\mathrm{i}\,\mathbf{B}\wedge r\;p_{r}+\frac{1}{4}|\mathbf{B}\wedge r|^{2}
. Using (
5.6
) and (
A.1
), we estimate the first term on the right side of (
6.23
) by

\displaystyle\|[\pi_{r}^{2}-p_{r}^{2}]\sigma_{0}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant B\,\|\,|\cdot|\nabla\alpha_{*}\|_{2}\|\Psi_{>}\|_{2}+B^{2}\|\,|\cdot|^{2}\alpha_{*}\|_{2}\|\Psi_{>}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}B^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}.

(6.24)

To estimate the second term in (
6.23
), we use Hölder’s inequality in (
2.1
) and find

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\int_{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}\frac{\mathrm{d}w}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\,f(w)\;\frac{1}{w+\mu-\pi_{r}^{2}}[\pi_{r}^{2}-p_{r}^{2}]\frac{1}{w+\mu-p_{r}^{2}}\sigma_{0}\Bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-180.0pt\leqslant\int_{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}\frac{\mathrm{d}|w|}{2\pi}\,|f(w)|\,\Bigl\|\frac{1}{w+\mu-\pi_{r}^{2}}\Bigr\|_{\infty}\Bigl\|[\pi_{r}^{2}-p_{r}^{2}]\frac{1}{w+\mu-p_{r}^{2}}\sigma_{0}\Bigr\|_{2},

where
\mathrm{d}|w|=\mathrm{d}t\;|w^{\prime}(t)|
. Eq. (
6.19
) implies that the operator norm of the magnetic resolvent is uniformly bounded for
w\in{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}
. Since the function
f
is exponentially decaying along the speaker path it suffices to prove a bound on the last factor that is uniform for
w\in{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}
. We have

\displaystyle[\pi_{r}^{2}-p_{r}^{2}]\frac{1}{w+\mu-p_{r}^{2}}\sigma_{0}(X,r)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}s\;[\pi_{r}^{2}-p_{r}^{2}]g_{0}^{w}(r-s)\alpha_{*}(s)\Psi_{>}(X),

which implies

\displaystyle\Bigl\|[\pi_{r}^{2}-p_{r}^{2}]\frac{1}{w+\mu-p_{r}^{2}}\sigma_{0}\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Psi_{>}\|_{2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\,\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}s\;|[\pi_{r}^{2}-p_{r}^{2}]g_{0}^{w}(r-s)\alpha_{*}(s)|\Bigr|^{2}.

(6.25)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}r\;\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}s\;|[\pi_{r}^{2}-p_{r}^{2}]g_{0}^{w}(r-s)\alpha_{*}(s)|\Bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-195.0pt\leqslant CB^{2}\bigl(\|\,|\cdot|\nabla g_{0}^{w}\|_{1}^{2}\;\|\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\nabla g_{0}^{w}\|_{1}^{2}\;\|\,|\cdot|\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\,|\cdot|^{2}g_{0}^{w}\|_{1}^{2}\;\|\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|g_{0}^{w}\|_{1}^{2}\;\|\,|\cdot|^{2}\alpha_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr).

The right side is uniformly bounded for
w\in{\hskip 0.7pt\text{\raisebox{-1.7pt}{\scalebox{1.4}{\faIconFromMacro{faVolumeOff}}}\hskip 0.7pt}}
by (
6.19
) and (
A.1
). In combination with (
5.6
) and (
6.25
), this implies

\displaystyle\Bigl\|[\pi_{r}^{2}-p_{r}^{2}]\frac{1}{w+\mu-p_{r}^{2}}\sigma_{0}\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant CB^{2}\;\|\Psi_{>}\|_{2}^{2}\leqslant C\varepsilon^{-1}B^{4}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}.

Using this and (
6.24
), we read off (
6.22
). Finally, we apply Proposition
3.2
to estimate
\|\eta_{\perp}\|_{2}
in (
6.21
), which proves part (a).

To prove part (b), we start by noting that

\displaystyle|\langle\eta_{\perp},(U-1)K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}\sigma_{0}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\,|r|\eta_{\perp}\|_{2}\;\|\,|r|^{-1}(U-1)K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}\,\sigma_{0}\|_{2}.

A bound for the left factor on the right side is provided by Proposition
3.2
. To estimate the right factor, we use (
5.6
), (
A.1
) and the operator inequality in (
4.99
), which implies
|U-1|^{2}\leqslant 3r^{2}\Pi_{X}^{2}
, and find

\displaystyle\|\,|r|^{-1}(U-1)K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}\,\sigma_{0}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}\alpha_{*}\|_{2}\;\|\Pi\Psi_{>}\|_{2}\leqslant CB\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}.

This proves part (b).

For part (c), we estimate

\displaystyle|\langle\eta_{\perp},UK_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}(U^{*}-1)\sigma_{0}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant\bigl\|\sqrt{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}}\,U^{*}\eta_{\perp}\bigr\|_{2}\;\bigl\|\sqrt{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}}\,(U^{*}-1)\sigma_{0}\bigr\|_{2}

(6.26)

and note that
K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}\leqslant C(1+\pi_{r}^{2})
implies

\displaystyle\bigl\|\sqrt{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}}(U^{*}-1)\sigma_{0}\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\langle\sigma_{0},(U-1)K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}(U^{*}-1)\sigma_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|(U^{*}-1)\sigma_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+C\|\pi_{r}(U^{*}-1)\sigma_{0}\|_{2}^{2}.

(6.27)

Using the bound for
|U-1|^{2}
in part (b), (
5.6
) and (
A.1
), we see that the first term is bounded by
C\||r|\alpha_{*}\Pi_{X}\Psi_{>}\|^{2}\leqslant CB^{2}
. Lemma
5.11
allows us to write

\displaystyle\pi_{r}(U^{*}-1)

\displaystyle=(U^{*}-1)\widetilde{\pi}_{r}+\frac{1}{2}U^{*}\Pi_{X}-\frac{1}{4}\mathbf{B}\wedge r.

Accordingly, we have

\displaystyle\|\pi_{r}(U^{*}-1)\sigma_{0}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-50.0pt\leqslant C\bigl(\|\,|r|p_{r}\alpha_{*}\Pi_{X}\Psi_{>}\|_{2}^{2}+B\,\|\,|r|^{2}\alpha_{*}\Pi_{X}\Psi_{>}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\alpha_{*}\Pi_{X}\Psi_{>}\|_{2}^{2}+B\|\,|r|\alpha_{*}\Psi_{>}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip-50.0pt\leqslant C\bigl(B^{2}+\varepsilon^{-1}B^{3}\bigr)\leqslant CB^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}.

We conclude that the right side of (
6.27
) is bounded by
CB^{2}\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}
.

With
K_{T}(p)\leqslant C(1+p^{2})
we see that the first factor on the right side of (
6.26
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\bigl\|\sqrt{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}}\,U^{*}\eta_{\perp}\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\langle\eta_{\perp},UK_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}U^{*}\eta_{\perp}\rangle\leqslant C\|\eta_{\perp}\|_{2}^{2}+C\|\pi_{r}U^{*}\eta_{\perp}\|_{2}^{2}.

From Lemma
5.11
we know that
\pi_{r}U^{*}=U^{*}[\widetilde{\pi}_{r}+\frac{1}{2}\Pi_{X}]
, and hence

\displaystyle\bigl\|\sqrt{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}^{r}}\,U^{*}\eta_{\perp}\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\bigl(\|\eta_{\perp}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\widetilde{\pi}_{r}\eta_{\perp}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\Pi_{X}\eta_{\perp}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)\leqslant C\varepsilon B^{2}\,\|\Psi\|_{H_{\mathrm{mag}}^{1}(Q_{B})}^{2}.

This proves part (c) and ends the proof of the Lemma
6.2
.
∎

— Appendix —

Appendix A
Estimates on Eigenvalues and Eigenfunctions of
K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-V

In this section, we investigate the low lying eigenvalues of
K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-V
and its ground state wave function. Our analysis is carried out at
T=T_{\mathrm{c}}
and we omit
T_{\mathrm{c}}
from the notation throughout the appendix. The goal is to prove the following result.

Proposition A.1
.

Let Assumptions
1.3
and
1.5
hold. There is a constant
B_{0}>0
such that for any
0\leqslant B\leqslant B_{0}
the following holds. Let
e_{0}^{B}
and
e_{1}^{B}
denote the lowest and next to lowest eigenvalue of
K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-V
. Then:

(a)

|e_{0}^{B}|\leqslant CB
,

(b)

K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-V
has a uniform spectral gap above
e_{0}^{B}
, i.e.,
e_{1}^{B}-e_{0}^{B}\geqslant\kappa>0
.

(c)

Let
\alpha_{*}
be the eigenfunction in (
1.15
) and let
\alpha_{*}^{B}
be an eigenfunction corresponding to
e_{0}^{B}
such that
\langle\alpha_{*}^{B},V\alpha_{*}\rangle
is real and nonnegative for all
0\leqslant B\leqslant B_{0}
. Then,

\displaystyle\|\alpha_{*}^{B}-\alpha_{*}\|_{2}+\|\pi^{2}(\alpha_{*}^{B}-\alpha_{*})\|_{2}\leqslant CB.

(d)

With
P_{B}:=|\alpha_{*}^{B}\rangle\langle\alpha_{*}^{B}|
and
P:=|\alpha_{*}\rangle\langle\alpha_{*}|
and with
\alpha_{*}^{B}
and
\alpha_{*}
as in part (c), we have

\displaystyle\|P_{B}-P\|_{\infty}+\|\pi^{2}(P_{B}-P)\|_{\infty}\leqslant CB.

Remark A.2
.

We emphasize that this appendix is the only place in the paper where the assumption
V\geqslant 0
is used. It simplifies our analysis because it implies that the Birman–Schwinger operator
V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}[K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e]^{-1}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
is self-adjoint. However, for the statement of Proposition
A.1
to be true, it is not necessary that
V
has a sign. In fact, with the help of a Combes–Thomas estimate for the resolvent kernel of
K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}-V
it is possible to show that Proposition
A.1
also holds for potentials
V
without a definite sign. This approach requires more effort, and we therefore refrain from giving a general proof here. It can be found in
[
42
, Chapter 6]
.

Let us recall the decay properties of the eigenfunction
\alpha_{*}
corresponding to the lowest eigenvalue of the operator
K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}-V
. Since
\alpha_{*}=K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{-1}V\alpha_{*}
and
V\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, we immediately have
\alpha_{*}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Furthermore, for any
\nu\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}
, by
[
19
, Proposition 2]
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}x\;\bigl[|x^{\nu}\alpha_{*}(x)|^{2}+|x^{\nu}\nabla\alpha_{*}(x)|^{2}\bigr]<\infty.

(A.1)

In fact, more regularity of
\alpha_{*}
is known, see
[
19
, Appendix A]
but (
A.1
) is all we use in this paper. Before we give the proof of Proposition
A.1
in Section
A.2
below, we prove two preparatory statements.

Let
e\in(-\infty,2T_{\mathrm{c}})
and denote the kernel of the resolvent
(e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}})^{-1}
by
\mathcal{G}^{e}(x-y)
.

Lemma A.3
.

For
e\in(-\infty,2T_{\mathrm{c}})
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
the functions
|\cdot|^{k}\mathcal{G}^{e}
and
|\cdot|^{k}\nabla\mathcal{G}^{e}
belong to
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

Proof.

The function
(e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}(p))^{-1}
and its derivatives belong to
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\|\,|\cdot|^{k}\mathcal{G}^{e}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leqslant\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathrm{d}x\;\bigl|\frac{|x|^{k}}{1+|x|^{k+2}}\bigr|^{2}\Bigr)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\;\|(1+|\cdot|^{k+2})\mathcal{G}^{e}\|_{2}<\infty.

(A.2)

This proves the first claim and the second follows from a similar argument.
∎

A.1
Phase approximation for
K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}

Proposition A.4
.

Let
V
and
|\cdot|^{2}V
belong to
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. There is
B_{0}>0
such that for
0\leqslant B\leqslant B_{0}
and
e\in(-\infty,2T_{\mathrm{c}})
, we have

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\Bigl[\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}}-\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}\Bigr]V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Bigr\|_{\infty}+\Bigl\|\pi^{2}\Bigl[\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}}-\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}\Bigr]V\Bigr\|_{\infty}\leqslant C_{e}B.

(A.3)

Proof.

To prove this result, we apply a phase approximation to the operator
K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}
. We pursue the strategy that we used in the proof of Lemma
4.5
and define

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{B}^{e}(x,y):=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathbf{B}}{2}\cdot(x\wedge y)}\;\mathcal{G}^{e}(x-y).

(A.4)

Let
\mathcal{S}_{B}^{e}
be the operator defined by the kernel
\mathcal{S}_{B}^{e}(x,y)
. We claim that

\displaystyle(e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B})\mathcal{S}_{B}^{e}=\mathbbs 1-\mathcal{T}_{B}^{e}

(A.5)

with the operator
\mathcal{T}_{B}^{e}
defined by the kernel

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{B}^{e}(x,y):=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathbf{B}}{2}\cdot(x\wedge y)}\bigl[(K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}(\pi_{x,y})-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}})\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}\bigr](x,y)

(A.6)

and
\pi_{x,y}=-\mathrm{i}\nabla_{x}+\mathbf{A}(x-y)
. To prove (
A.5
), it is sufficient to note that

\displaystyle K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}\;\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathbf{B}}{2}\cdot(x\wedge y)}=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathbf{B}}{2}\cdot(x\wedge y)}\;K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}(\pi_{x,y}).

Using Lemma
6.4
and Lemma
A.3
, a straightforward computation shows that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{B}^{e}\|_{\infty}\leqslant C_{e}\,B

(A.7)

holds for
B
small enough.

With the operator
\mathcal{S}_{B}^{e}
in (
A.4
) we write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}}-\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}=\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}}-\mathcal{S}_{B}^{e}+\mathcal{S}_{B}^{e}-\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}

(A.8)

For the first term (
A.5
) implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}}-\mathcal{S}_{B}^{e}=\mathcal{S}_{B}^{e}\;\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(\mathcal{T}_{B}^{e})^{n}

and since
\mathcal{S}_{B}^{e}
is a bounded operator with norm bounded by
\|\mathcal{G}^{e}\|_{1}
, (
A.7
) yields

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}}-\mathcal{S}_{B}^{e}\Bigr\|_{\infty}\leqslant C_{e}B.

To estimate the second term on the right side of (
A.8
), we use
|\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{\mathbf{B}}{2}\cdot(x\wedge y)}-1|\leqslant B|x-y||y|
and bound the kernel of this term by

\displaystyle\bigl|\bigl[\mathcal{S}_{B}^{e}-\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}\bigr]V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(x,y)\bigr|

\displaystyle\leqslant B\;|x-y||\mathcal{G}^{e}(x-y)|\;|y||V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(y)|.

We further bound
|y||V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(y)|\leqslant\|\,|\cdot|^{2}V\|_{\infty}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
, which shows that

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\Bigl[\mathcal{S}_{B}^{e}-\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}\Bigr]V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Bigr\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leqslant B\;\|\,|\cdot|^{2}V\|_{\infty}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\;\|\,|\cdot|\mathcal{G}^{e}\|_{1}\leqslant C_{e}B.

This completes the proof of the first estimate in (
A.3
).

To prove the second estimate, we note that

\displaystyle\pi^{2}\Bigl[\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}}-\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}\Bigr]V=\pi^{2}\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}}[K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}]\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.

Since
\pi^{2}(e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B})^{-1}
is a bounded function of
\pi^{2}
, we know that the operator norm of the operator in the above equation is uniformly bounded in
B
. Thus, it suffices to show that
[K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}]\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}V
satisfies the claimed operator norm bound. To this end, we use (
6.23
) and obtain two terms. Since
\pi^{2}-p^{2}=\mathbf{B}\wedge x\cdot p+\frac{1}{4}|\mathbf{B}\wedge x|^{2}
, the estimate for the first term reads

\displaystyle\bigl[(\pi^{2}-p^{2})\frac{1}{e-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\bigr](x,y)\leqslant\bigl[B\cdot|x||\nabla\mathcal{G}^{e}(x-y)|+B^{2}\;|x|^{2}|\mathcal{G}^{e}(x-y)|\bigr]|V(y)|.

The
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
-norm in
x-y
of the right side is bounded by

\displaystyle CB\,\bigl[\bigl(\|\,|\cdot|\nabla\mathcal{G}^{e}\|_{1}+\|\,|\cdot|^{2}\mathcal{G}^{e}\|_{1}\bigr)\|V\|_{\infty}+\|\nabla\mathcal{G}^{e}\|_{1}\,\|\,|\cdot|V\|_{\infty}+\|\mathcal{G}^{e}\|_{1}\,\|\,|\cdot|^{2}V\|_{\infty}\bigr],

which is finite by Lemma
A.3
. With the help of (
6.19
) the remaining term can bounded similarly. This proves the claim.
∎

A.2
Asymptotics for eigenvalues and eigenfunctions

We are now prepared to give the proof of Proposition
A.1
.

Proof of Proposition
A.1
.

We start with the upper bound of part (a). By the variational principle for
e_{0}^{B}
we have

\displaystyle e_{0}^{B}\leqslant\langle\alpha_{*},(K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-V)\alpha_{*}\rangle=\langle\alpha_{*},(K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}-V)\alpha_{*}\rangle+\langle\alpha_{*},(K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}})\alpha_{*}\rangle,

(A.9)

where the first term on the right side equals
0
by the definition of
\alpha_{*}
. We use (
6.19
), Lemma
6.4
, and (
A.1
), and argue as in the proof of (
6.24
) to see that the second term is bounded by
CB
.

In the next step, we show the lower bound of part (a) and part (b) at the same time. Thus, for
n=0,1
we aim to show

\displaystyle e_{n}^{B}\geqslant e_{n}^{0}-C_{n}B

(A.10)

for the lowest and next-to-lowest eigenvalue
e_{0}^{B}
and
e_{1}^{B}
, respectively. For notational convenience, we give the proof for general
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and we order the eigenvalues
e_{n}^{B}
increasingly. Let
\alpha_{n}^{B}
be the eigenfunction to
e_{n}^{B}
for
n\geqslant 0
and note that
\alpha_{*}^{B}=\alpha_{0}^{B}
.

Now, we switch to the Birman–Schwinger picture:
e_{n}^{B}
being the
(n+1)
-st to smallest eigenvalue of
K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-V
is equivalent to 1 being the
(n+1)
-st to largest eigenvalue of the Birman–Schwinger operator
V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}^{B})^{-1}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
corresponding to
e_{n}^{B}
. Accordingly, the min-max principle, see, e.g.,
[
40
, Theorem 12.1 (5)]
, implies

\displaystyle 1=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{0},\ldots,u_{n}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\\
u_{i}\perp u_{j},\;i\neq j\end{subarray}}\min\Bigl\{\Bigl\langle\Phi,V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}^{B}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Phi\Bigr\rangle:\Phi\in\mathrm{span}\{u_{0},\ldots,u_{n}\},\;\|\Phi\|_{2}=1\Bigr\}.

(A.11)

We obtain a lower bound on (
A.11
) by choosing the functions
u_{i}
,
i=0,\ldots,n
as the first
n+1
orthonormal eigenfunctions
\varphi_{i}^{B}
satisfying

\displaystyle V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}^{B}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\varphi_{i}^{B}=\eta_{i}^{B}\,\varphi_{i}^{B},\qquad\qquad i=0,\ldots,n,

(A.12)

where
\eta_{i}^{B}\geqslant 1
denote the first
n
eigenvalues of the Birman–Schwinger operator in (
A.12
) ordered decreasingly. In particular, we have
\eta_{n}^{B}=1
, as well as the relations
\varphi_{n}^{B}=V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\alpha_{n}^{B}
and
\alpha_{n}^{B}=(K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}^{B})^{-1}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\varphi_{n}^{B}
.

Denote
e_{n}:=e_{n}^{0}
and apply the resolvent equations to write

\displaystyle V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}^{B}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle=V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}-e_{n}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+(e_{n}^{B}-e_{n})\,\mathcal{Q}_{n}^{B}+\mathcal{R}_{n}^{B}

(A.13)

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{n}^{B}

\displaystyle:=V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}}\,\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}^{B}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{n}^{B}

\displaystyle:=V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Bigl[\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}}-\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}-e_{n}}\Bigr]V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.

By Proposition
A.4
, we have
\|\mathcal{R}_{n}^{B}\|_{\infty}\leqslant C_{n}B
. Furthermore, we may assume without loss of generality that
e_{n}^{B}\leqslant e_{n}
, because otherwise there is nothing to prove. We combine this with (
A.11
) for
B=0
and (
A.13
), which yields

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle\geqslant\min\Bigl\{\Bigl\langle\Phi,V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}^{B}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Phi\Bigr\rangle:\Phi\in\mathrm{span}\{\varphi_{0}^{B},\ldots,\varphi_{n}^{B}\},\;\|\Phi\|_{2}=1\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt-[e_{n}^{B}-e_{n}]\min\bigl\{\langle\Phi,\mathcal{Q}_{n}^{B}\Phi\rangle:\Phi\in\mathrm{span}\{\varphi_{0}^{B},\ldots,\varphi_{n}^{B}\},\;\|\Phi\|_{2}=1\bigr\}-C_{n}B.

(A.14)

We observe that the first term on the right side equals 1. To be able to conclude, we therefore need to show that there is a constant
c>0
, independent of
B
, such that

\displaystyle\min\bigl\{\langle\Phi,\mathcal{Q}_{n}^{B}\Phi\rangle:\Phi\in\mathrm{span}\{\varphi_{0}^{B},\ldots,\varphi_{n}^{B}\}\bigr\}\geqslant c.

(A.15)

Then, (
A.14
) implies
-[e_{n}^{B}-e_{n}]\leqslant C_{n}B
, which proves (
A.10
).

We will prove that (
A.15
) holds with
c=\|V\|_{\infty}^{-1}
. To that end, we write

\displaystyle\langle\Phi,\mathcal{Q}_{n}^{B}\Phi\rangle

\displaystyle=\Bigl\langle\Phi,V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}^{B}}(K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}^{B})\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}^{B}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Phi\Bigr\rangle,

apply
-e_{n}^{B}\geqslant-e_{n}
, and infer

\displaystyle\langle\Phi,\mathcal{Q}_{n}^{B}\Phi\rangle

\displaystyle\geqslant\Bigl\|\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}^{B}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Phi\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}.

Since
\Phi\in\mathrm{span}\{\varphi_{0}^{B},\ldots,\varphi_{n}^{B}\}
, there are coefficients
c_{i}^{B}\in\mathbb{C}
,
i=0,\ldots,n
such that we have
\Phi=\sum_{i=0}^{n}c_{i}^{B}\varphi_{i}^{B}
. We use the eigenvalue equation in (
A.12
) for
\varphi_{i}^{B}
as well as
\langle\varphi_{i}^{B},\varphi_{j}^{B}\rangle=\delta_{i,j}
to see that

\displaystyle\|V\|_{\infty}\Bigl\|\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}^{B}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Phi\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geqslant\Bigl\langle\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}^{B}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Phi,V\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{n}^{B}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Phi\Bigr\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=0}^{n}\overline{c_{i}^{B}}c_{j}^{B}\,\eta_{i}^{B}\eta_{j}^{B}\,\langle\varphi_{i}^{B},\varphi_{j}^{B}\rangle=\sum_{i=0}^{n}|c_{i}^{B}|^{2}\;(\eta_{i}^{B})^{2}\geqslant 1.

Here, we used that
\eta_{i}\geqslant 1
and
\|\Phi\|_{2}=1
. This shows (
A.15
) and completes the proof of (
A.10
).

The case
n=0
in (
A.10
) yields the lower bound of part (a), since
e_{0}=0
. For
n=1
, we have
e_{1}=\kappa
, showing part (b) with the help of part (a).

To prove part (c), we write
\varphi_{0}:=\varphi_{0}^{0}
. The chosen phase of
\varphi_{0}^{B}
in the lemma is such that
\langle\varphi_{0},\varphi_{0}^{B}\rangle
is real and nonnegative. We write
\varphi_{0}^{B}=a_{B}\varphi_{0}+b_{B}\Phi
with
\langle\Phi,\varphi_{0}\rangle=0
,
\|\Phi\|_{2}=1
and
|a_{B}|^{2}+|b_{B}|^{2}=1
. By construction,
a_{B}=\langle\varphi_{0},\varphi_{0}^{B}\rangle
. Furthermore, by (
A.13
),

\displaystyle 1=\bigl\langle\varphi_{0}^{B},V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{0}^{B}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\varphi_{0}^{B}\bigr\rangle=\bigl\langle\varphi_{0}^{B},V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\varphi_{0}^{B}\bigr\rangle+\langle\varphi_{0}^{B},\mathcal{T}_{B}\varphi_{0}^{B}\rangle

(A.16)

with
\mathcal{T}_{B}:=e_{0}^{B}\,Q_{0}^{B}+\mathcal{R}_{0}^{B}
. Thus,

\displaystyle 1\leqslant a_{B}^{2}+|b_{B}|^{2}\bigl\langle\Phi,V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Phi\bigr\rangle+2a_{B}\Re\bigl[b_{B}\bigl\langle\varphi_{0},V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Phi\bigr\rangle\bigr]+\|\mathcal{T}_{B}\|_{\infty}.

By parts (a) and (b) and Proposition
A.4
, we know that
\|\mathcal{T}_{B}\|_{\infty}\leqslant CB
. Furthermore, the term in square brackets vanishes, since
V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{-1}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\varphi_{0}=\varphi_{0}
and
\langle\varphi_{0},\Phi\rangle=0
. Using the orthogonality of
\Phi
and
\varphi_{0}
once more as well as the fact that 1 is the largest eigenvalue of
V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{-1}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
, we see that there is an
\eta<1
such that
\langle\Phi,V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{-1}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Phi\rangle\leqslant\eta
. It follows that

\displaystyle 1\leqslant a_{B}^{2}+|b_{B}|^{2}\eta+CB.

Since
a_{B}^{2}+|b_{B}|^{2}=1
, this implies
|b_{B}|^{2}\leqslant CB
as well as
a_{B}^{2}\geqslant 1-CB
. Since
a_{B}\geqslant 0
, we infer
1-a_{B}\leqslant CB
.

The next step is to improve the estimate on
b_{B}
to
|b_{B}|\leqslant CB
. To this end, we combine the two eigenvalue equations of
\varphi_{0}^{B}
and
\varphi_{0}
. With
\mathcal{T}_{B}
as in (
A.16
), we find

\displaystyle\varphi_{0}^{B}-\varphi_{0}

\displaystyle=V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(\varphi_{0}^{B}-\varphi_{0})+\mathcal{T}_{B}\varphi_{0}^{B}.

Testing this against
\Phi
, we obtain

\displaystyle b_{B}=\langle\Phi,\varphi_{0}^{B}-\varphi_{0}\rangle=\bigl\langle V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Phi,\varphi_{0}^{B}-\varphi_{0}\bigr\rangle+\langle\Phi,\mathcal{T}_{B}\varphi_{0}\rangle.

We apply Cauchy-Schwarz on the right side and use that
\|V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}^{-1}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Phi\|_{2}\leqslant\eta
, which follows from the orthogonality of
\Phi
and
\varphi_{0}
. We also use
\|\varphi_{0}^{B}-\varphi_{0}\|_{2}\leqslant(1-a_{B})+|b_{B}|
. This implies

\displaystyle|b_{B}|\leqslant\eta\,(1-a_{B})+\eta\,|b_{B}|+CB,

from which we conclude that
|b_{B}|\leqslant CB
.

It remains to use these findings to prove the claimed bounds for
\alpha_{*}^{B}-\alpha_{*}
. According to the Birman–Schwinger correspondence, we have
\alpha_{*}^{B}=(K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{0}^{B})^{-1}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\varphi_{0}^{B}
. Thus, since
\varphi_{0}=V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\alpha_{*}
,

\displaystyle\alpha_{*}^{B}-\alpha_{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{0}^{B}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\bigl[a_{B}\varphi_{0}+b_{B}\Phi\bigr]-\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\varphi_{0}

\displaystyle=a_{B}\Bigl[\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{0}^{B}}-\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}\Bigr]V\alpha_{*}+b_{B}\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}},B}-e_{0}^{B}}V^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Phi-(1-a_{B})\frac{1}{K_{T_{\mathrm{c}}}}V\alpha_{*}.

(A.17)

The proof of the norm estimates for
\alpha_{*}^{B}-\alpha
and
\pi^{2}(\alpha_{*}^{B}-\alpha_{*})
is obtained from Lemma
A.3
, Proposition
A.4
, and the estimates of part (a) on
e_{0}^{B}
.

Part (d) follows from part (c). This ends the proof of Proposition
A.1
.
∎
